Projected subgradient:
- Normalize a sum=1.
- Compute c=conv(a,a) via FFT.
- Select active set: indices k where c[k] >= M - eps or top K.
- Compute grad = sum_{k active} weight_k * shift(a, k) (where shift value at j = a[k-j] if valid). weights maybe c[k] relative.
- Update a -= lr * grad; clip; normalize; line search (if M decreased accept).
- Add random perturbations to escape.

Projected subgradient for max(c): Since c can have many active peaks. We can compute a "smooth proxy" S_p(c)=p * logsumexp(c/tau) or sum of top K. But maybe the non-smooth subgradient with random restarts can work.

A simpler heuristic: Iteratively reduce the largest convolution value by solving a local optimization over a small set of indices. If c[k] is peak, c[k]=∑_i a_i a_{k-i}. Think of variable x_i = a_i * a_{k-i} pairs. To reduce c[k] without changing sum, we can lower a_i or a_{k-i}. Because c[k] is sum of products, a gradient descent step on c[k] alone: a_i -= η * ∂c/∂a_i = η * a_{k-i}. Normalizing. This tends to make a anti-correlated with its shifted self: high values where a[k-i] low. This may produce asymmetric shapes.

We can accelerate by applying many steps for the current peak, then recompute. This is like coordinate descent. We can also randomize peak lag among top few.

Pseudo:
```
a = initial normalized length m
for iterations:
  c = fft_conv(a)
  k = argmax c
  M=c[k]
  # choose step size adaptive
  g = np.zeros(m)
  for j in valid: g[j] = a[k-j] (with if 0<=k-j<m)
  # maybe only where g large
  a_new = a - lr * g / (m?)? Need scale.
  normalize, clip
  if M_new < M: accept else reduce lr
```
At uniform, g constant, normalize no change. Add random noise.

But to get below 2, need a shape. Random noise + subgradient maybe shape into something. Let's simulate mentally: uniform plus small random, peak center. g at center is a_{n-1-j} ≈ reversed random. Update subtract reversed a: a_new = a - lr * reverse(a) + noise. This creates asymmetry: high where original low, low where original high, making a more anti-symmetric relative to center. That is good! Normalize. Repeating may converge to a and -a? But nonnegativity. The long-run fixed point of update a = normalize(a - reverse(a)) is sequences with a_i = a_{n-1-i}? Wait if a - reverse(a) positive? For nonnegative, subtracting reverse can make negatives clipped. It may converge to a supported at one side? Let's see. If a is symmetric, g=a (if k center and reverse symmetric), update zero. If a has asymmetry, high values where reverse low remain, low where reverse high may be reduced. This may create a monotone ramp? Could help.

But if update too strong, may shrink to endpoints causing high peaks. Need careful line search.

Alternatively, use a "score" at positions: For peak lag k, define penalty p_i = a[k-i] (mirror). We want allocate mass to low p_i. Algorithm:
```
a = initial positive (e.g., random lognormal or uniform+noise)
normalize
for it:
   c=conv(a); k=argmax
   M=c[k]
   p = mirror k: p[i]=a[k-i] if valid else 0
   # new weights: a_new[i] = a[i] * (1 - eta * p[i]/M?) or exp(-eta p[i])
   # To avoid zeros, use a_new = a * (1 - eta * p/(p.max()+eps))
   a_new = clip(0)
   normalize
   if improves accept else reduce eta, add random
```


Alternative direct sequence optimization may be more flexible. Let's design a robust direct stochastic search that can be run after low-dim.

Direct search ideas:
- Use "simulated annealing" with block multiplicative updates. For a length N=2048, evaluate score, perturb a block by multiplying by exp(noise), maybe symmetrize? Need target <1.5. Starting from best shape, local search can improve.
- Use coordinate-ascent on peak lag:
   For current peak k, we want reduce c[k]. Since c[k]=∑ a_i a_{k-i}. Let p_i=a[k-i] (valid). Approximate c as inner product of a and p. Given sum a=1,a>=0, minimize inner product with fixed p by putting mass where p small. But changing a also changes p? p is current mirror, use linearized update. The optimal a' for linearized objective c≈a·p + constant? Actually c = a·p. To minimize a·p subject sum=1,a>=0, set all mass at argmin p, which is too sparse. Instead do exponential tilting: a' ∝ a exp(-η p), or a' ∝ (a + small) / (1+η p). This gradually shifts mass to low p.
   This is like multiplicative weights. We can iterate for the peak lag until c decreases, then recompute top lag.
   Pseudo:
```
for step in range(...):
   c=conv(a)
   k=argmax
   p = shift_moved(a,k)
   # normalize p maybe
   a_new = a * (1 - eta * p / (p.max()+eps)) # simple
   # add small noise
   a_new = clip
   if sum>0 normalize
   if score < best: update else eta decay
```
This may produce shapes. Need handle p zero at edges; okay.

Could also solve for a new vector b minimizing ∑ b_i p_i subject to convex constraints like large l1? The LP state-of-art is exactly that: with fixed a, find b maximizing sum subject to a*b <= M, not minimizing p·b. Different.

Maybe use "shifted adversarial" optimization: We want min max_k a * shift_k(a). This is a min-max bilinear game. We can use a multiplicative weights / mirror descent for minimax: Maintain distribution a (sum=1). The adversary chooses lag k maximizing convolution. The value V_{a,k}=a·shift_k(a) (bilinear? Actually V = ∑ a_i a_{k-i} = a^T S_k a, not bilinear in separate a,b unless we fix one copy). If we treat x = a (sum=1) and y = a maybe, min_x max_k x^T S_k x. Nonconvex.

But we can use a self-play: Given current a, adversary k. Update a by mirror descent with gradient of x^T S_k x = 2 S_k x. Multiply by exp(-η S_k x). This is similar to above.

This has potential to converge to game equilibrium. Starting from symmetric uniform, gradient S_k x constant, no move; add noise. Then asymmetry emerges. We can implement with adaptive η and random lags (top few). This is like multiplicative weights for nonconvex but might work.

Let's detail `fft_score`. For normalized a length n, using FFT:
```
def fft_score(seq):
    a=np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    a=a/s
    if n < 128:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       L=1 << max(1, (2*n-1)-1).bit_length()? Need next power >= 2n-1. `1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()`? If 2n-1 is power of two, bit_length gives p+1? Example 8.bit_length=4, 1<<4=16 >8. Need if x & (x-1). Use:
       L=1
       while L < 2*n-1: L <<=1
       F=np.fft.fft(a, L)
       c=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:2*n-1]
    M=c.max()
    return float(2*n*M)
```
Since sum=1.

`peak_lag_penalty(a, k):`
```
def lag_penalty(a, k):
   n=a.size
   p=np.zeros(n)
   # j from max(0,k-n+1) to min(n-1,k)
   lo=max(0, k - n + 1)
   hi=min(n-1, k)
   if lo <= hi:
       # p[lo:hi+1] = a[k - j], j increasing -> index decreasing
       p[lo:hi+1] = a[hi-lo:-? reversed]
```
Need assign correctly: for j=lo..hi, idx=k-j ranges k-lo down to k-hi. So `p[lo:hi+1] = a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]`? Let's test with n=5,k=4 (center). lo=0,hi=4. k-lo=4, k-hi=0. We want p[0]=a[4], p[4]=a[0]. Slice `a[4:0:-1]` gives [a4,a3,a2,a1] not a0. Need include hi: `a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]`? 4: -1: -1 gives [4,3,2,1,0] yes if stop -1. But if k-hi=0, stop -1 good. General hi can be k, then k-hi=0. If lo=0. If k small e.g., k=1,n=5: lo=0,hi=1. k-lo=1, k-hi=0, slice a[1:-1:-1] = [a1,a0]? gives [a1,a0] length2, p[0]=a1 (k-j=1 for j0), p[1]=a0 (k-j=0), good. If k>n-1, e.g., k=7,n=5: lo=3,hi=4. k-lo=4, k-hi=3, slice a[4:2:-1]=[a4,a3], p[3]=a4 (7-3=4), p[4]=a3 (7-4=3), good. So:

```
p[lo:hi+1] = a[k-lo : k-hi-1 : -1]
```

`top_penalty(a, c, num_top)`:
```
def top_penalty(a,c,num_top):
   n=len(c); p=np.zeros(len(a))
   # choose top indices among first min(num_top,n)
   m=min(num_top,n)
   idx=np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
   # weights = c[idx] / c[idx].sum()
   for k in idx:
       w=c[k]
       if w>0:
          p += lag_penalty(a,k) * (w / wsum)
   return p
```
Weighted by current c. If wsum zero (all zero) return zeros.

Pseudo local loop:
```
def local_search(a, deadline, rng):
   a=normalize_nonneg(a)
   sc=fft_score(a); best=a.copy(); best_sc=sc
   eta=0.2
   stuck=0
   while time < deadline:
      c=compute_conv(a)
      M=c.max(); cur_sc=2*n*M
      if cur_sc < best_sc: best=a.copy(); best_sc=cur_sc
      # choose top penalty
      p=top_penalty(a,c,5)
      pmax=p.max()
      if pmax <= 0: break
      improved=False
      for eta_try in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*2):
          cand=a*np.exp(-eta_try*p/pmax)
          if rng.random()<0.5:
              cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.01,n))
          cand=normalize nonneg
          sc_try=fft_score(cand) # computes conv again
          if sc_try < best_sc - 1e-12:
              a=cand; eta=eta_try; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
          eta=max(0.02, eta*0.8)
          # random kick from best
          if stuck>20:
             cand=best * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,n)); normalize
             sc_try=fft_score(cand)
             if sc_try < best_sc: a=cand; best=...
          stuck+=1
      else: stuck=0
   return best,best_sc
```

We can wrap theta in trig_obj by `theta = np.clip(theta[:2*K], [-1.2,1.2] for amp, phases modulo?`. Amplitudes can be clipped; phases modulo 2π. This makes objective piecewise but okay.
```
def trig_seq(theta, n, K):
   theta=np.asarray(theta)
   amp=theta[0:K]; ph=theta[K:2*K]
   amp=np.clip(amp, -1.5,1.5); ph=ph % (2*np.pi)
   x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
   f=np.ones(n)
   for j in range(K):
       if abs(amp[j])>1e-12:
          f += amp[j]*np.sin(2*np.pi*(j+1)*x + ph[j])
   f=np.clip(f,0,1000)
   s=f.sum()
   if s<=1e-12: return np.ones(n)/n
   return f/s
```
Include a small constant offset to avoid all zeros? Already 1.

```
def lp_update(a, M):
   n=len(a); rows=2n-1
   A=np.zeros((rows,n))
   for j in range(n):
       # a[j] contributes to b_i? Constraint for k: sum_i a[i] b[k-i] <= M. Coefficient for b_j is sum_i a[i] where k-i=j => i=k-j. So A[k,j] = a[k-j] if 0<=k-j<n.
       # We can fill A[j: j+?]? For fixed b_j, it appears in constraints k = i+j for i such that i valid 0..n-1 => k=j..j+n-1, coefficient a[i]=a[k-j]. So A[j:j+n, j] = a[0:n] (length n) but rows up to 2n-2; slice j to j+n inclusive? For k=j..j+n-1, coefficient a[k-j]=a[0..n-1]. Yes length n. If j+n-1 may exceed rows? max j=n-1 => k up to 2n-2, rows length 2n-1, slice j:j+n okay length n.
       A[j:j+n, j] = a
   res=linprog(c=-ones, A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(rows,M), bounds=(0,None))
   if res.success: return res.x, res.fun? sum
```

LP function top-level:
```
def lp_best_b(a):
   n=a.size; M=max conv
   A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n, j] = a
   res=linprog(-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=np.full(2*n-1,M), bounds=(0,None), options={'presolve':True})
   if res.success: b=res.x; return b, float(res.fun*-1), M
   return None,0, M
```

Let's detail `trig_search` with time:
```
def trig_search(deadline, rng, k_terms=3):
    n_eval=256
    best_theta=None; best_score=inf; best_seq=None
    starts=[]
    # structured
    for comp in (0.5,0.7,0.9): starts.append([comp,0,0,0,0,0])
    starts.append([-0.7,0,0,0,0,0])
    for _ in range(30): random
    for theta0 in starts:
       if time>deadline-break
       # evaluate initial at n_eval=256
       sc=trig_obj(theta0,n_eval,k_terms)
       if sc<best_score: update
       # run minimize with maxiter depending on time: 150
       if HAVE_OPT:
          res=minimize(trig_obj, theta0, args=(n_eval,k_terms), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':200,'xatol':1e-3,'fatol':1e-5})
          if res.fun<best_score: update
       # also try theta at n_eval=1024 maybe
    # after all, refine best theta with one more minimize at n_eval=1024 maxiter=100
    # return best_theta and score at n_eval=1024
```

Constants:
`TARGET = 1.5027`

Helpers:
1 `def _next_pow2(m): L=1; while L<m: L<<=1; return L`
2 `def _normalize(a):`
```
a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
s=a.sum()
if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=1e-12:
   return None
return a/s
```
3 `def _conv(a):` assume normalized? It can normalize? We'll have separate.
```
def _autoconv(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float); n=a.size
   L=1 << int(np.ceil(np.log2(2*n-1)))? Use while.
   F=np.fft.fft(a,L)
   c=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:2*n-1]
   return c
```
For n<128 direct.
4 `def _score(a):`
```
a=_normalize(a)
if a is None: return np.inf
n=len(a)
if n<small: c=np.convolve(a,a)
else: c=_autoconv(a)
M=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else inf
return float(2*n*M)
```
Since normalized S=1.
5 `def _lag_penalty(a,k):` as above.
6 `def _top_penalty(a,c,ntop=5):`
```
p=np.zeros(a.size)
m=min(ntop, c.size)
idx=np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
wsum=float(c[idx].sum())
if wsum<=0: return p
for k in idx:
   p += (float(c[k])/wsum) * _lag_penalty(a,int(k))
return p
```
7 `def _trig_sequence(theta,n,K):`
```
theta=np.asarray(theta,float).ravel()
if theta.size<2*K:
   theta=np.pad(theta, (0,2*K-theta.size))
amp=theta[:K].copy(); ph=theta[K:2*K].copy()
amp=np.clip(amp,-1.5,1.5); ph=ph % (2*np.pi)
x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
f=np.ones(n)
for j in range(K):
   ampj=float(amp[j])
   if ampj != 0:
      f = f + ampj * np.sin((2*np.pi*(j+1))*x + ph[j])
f=np.clip(f,0,1000)
s=f.sum()
if s<=1e-12: return np.ones(n)/n
return f/s
```
8 `def _trig_obj(theta,n,K): return _score(_trig_sequence(theta,n,K))`
9 `def _step_sequence(params,n):`
```
p=params[0]; logr=params[1]
p=float(np.clip(p,0.02,0.98))
r=float(np.exp(np.clip(logr,-4,4)))
k=int(max(1,min(n-1,round(p*n))))
a=np.zeros(n); a[:k]=1.0; a[k:]=r
return _normalize or return normalized
```
10 `def _step_obj(params,n): return _score(_step_sequence(params,n))`
11 `def _ramp_sequence(params,n):` maybe
```
amp=np.clip(params[0],-1,1); g=np.clip(params[1],0.2,10)
x=np.arange(n)/float(n)
a=1 + amp*(x**g)
```
But mean not 1, normalized._score normalizes. Add optional. Not necessary.

```
a=np.asarray(x,float); a=np.clip(a,0,1000)+1e-8
s=a.sum()
if s<=... return inf

```

`def _local_search(a, deadline, rng):`
- Normalize a.
- n = len(a). If n < 32: maybe not; can run direct with np.convolve. But we likely call with 4096.
- best=a, best_score=_score(a).
- c,_ = score from `_compute_score_from_conv`? Implement `_score_from_normalized(a)` returns (score,c).
```
def _score_normalized(a):
   n=a.size
   if n<128: c=np.convolve(a,a)
   else: c=_autoconv(a)
   M=float(c.max()) if c.size else inf
   return float(2*n*M), c
```
- Loop:
```
eta=0.15; old_fail=0
while time.monotonic() < deadline:
   sc,c = _score_normalized(a)
   if sc < best_score:
      best_score=sc; best=a.copy()
   if not np.isfinite(sc) or c.max()<=0: break
   p=_top_penalty(a,c,5)
   pmax=p.max()
   if pmax<=1e-300:
      p=_lag_penalty(a,int(np.argmax(c)))
      pmax=p.max()
   if pmax<=...: break
   best_try=None
   for e in (eta, eta*0.5, eta*1.5):
      cand=a*np.exp(-e*(p/pmax))
      # noise
      if rng.random()<0.8:
          sig = 0.01 if sc < 1.6 else 0.03
          cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
      cand=np.clip(cand,1e-12,1000)
      s=cand.sum()
      if s<=...: continue
      cand/=s
      sc_try,_= _score_normalized(cand)
      if sc_try < best_score - 1e-12:
          a=cand; eta=e if e<1 else eta; best_sc=...; best_try=True; break
   if not best_try:
      eta=max(0.02, eta*0.75)
      old_fail +=1
      if old_fail > 30:
         # random restart from best
         cand=best*(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.2,n)))
         normalize; sc_try...
         if sc_try<best_score: a=cand; best...
         old_fail=0
         eta=0.15
   else:
      old_fail=0
```
Need ensure `eta` not too high causing underflow. `exp(-1.5)` okay.
This evaluates 1 current score + up to 3 candidates; okay.
But `eta*1.5` may >2; clip eta_try <=1.0.
We can reduce candidate evaluations by trying 2.

`def _interpolate(a,n):`

```
if len(a)==n: return a

x_new=np.linspace(0, len(a)-1, n)
y=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
return _normalize(y)
```

```
def _trig_search(deadline, rng, K=3):
    n_eval=256
    best_theta=np.zeros(2*K)
    best_score=np.inf
    # initial structured
    starts=[]
    starts.append(np.zeros(2*K))
    for amp in (-0.9,-0.6,-0.3,0.3,0.6,0.9):
       th=np.zeros(2*K); th[0]=amp; starts.append(th)
    for amp2 in (-0.5,0.5):
       th=np.zeros(2*K); th[0]=0.7; th[1]=amp2; starts.append(th)
    for _ in range(15):
       th=np.concatenate((rng.uniform(-1,1,K), rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,K)))
       starts.append(th)
    # evaluate
    for th in starts:
       sc=_trig_obj(th,n_eval,K)
       if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_theta=th.copy()
    # refine with minimize or random
    if HAVE_OPT:
       for th0 in starts[:12]:
          if time.monotonic() > deadline - 20: break
          res=minimize(_trig_obj, th0, args=(n_eval,K), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':80, 'xatol':1e-3, 'fatol':1e-5, 'adaptive': True})
          if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun) and res.fun < best_score:
             best_score=res.fun; best_theta=res.x.copy()
          # also evaluate res.x at 512 maybe
    else:
       for _ in range(20000):
          if time...
          th=np.concatenate((rng.uniform(-1,1,K), rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi,K)))
          sc=_trig_obj(th,n_eval,K)
          if sc<best: update
    # final refine best at 512
    if HAVE_OPT and time.monotonic() < deadline-30:
       res=minimize(_trig_obj, best_theta, args=(512,K), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':100,...})
       if finite update
    seq=_trig_sequence(best_theta, 1024, K)
    score=_score(seq)
    return seq, score, best_theta
```

Let's write `_score_normalized` returns c computed direct/FFT:
```
def _score_normalized(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n == 0: return np.inf, np.array([])
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12: return np.inf, np.array([])
    a=a/s
    if n < 128:
       c=np.convolve(a,a)
    else:
       L=1; m=2*n-1
       while L < m: L <<= 1
       F=np.fft.fft(a,L)
       c=np.fft.ifft(F*F).real[:m]
    if c.size == 0: return np.inf, c
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf, c
    return float(2.0*n*M), c
```
`_score` can just call `_score_normalized`.

For `_score` input maybe list; it will normalize inside. Good.

Let's draft code mentally and check syntax.

```
def _score_normalized(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    if n < 128:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        m = 2 * n - 1
        L = 1
        while L < m:
            L <<= 1
        F = np.fft.fft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.ifft(F * F).real[:m]
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf, c
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return np.inf, c
    return float(2.0 * n * M), c
```

`_score = lambda` not allowed. Define:
```
def _score(a):
    sc, _ = _score_normalized(a)
    return sc
```

`_lag_penalty`:
```
def _lag_penalty(a, k):
    n = a.size
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, k)
    if lo <= hi:
        j = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        p[lo:hi+1] = a[k - j]
    return p
```
If k-j could be negative? By lo/hi ensures 0<=k-j<n. Good.

`_top_penalty`:
```
def _top_penalty(a, c, ntop=5):
    p = np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    n = c.size
    if n == 0: return p
    m = min(ntop, n)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
    w = c[idx]
    wsum = float(np.sum(w))
    if wsum <= 0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
       return p
    for kk in idx:
       weight = float(kk? no)
```
Need weight = c[kk]/wsum. But idx is array. Use loop:
```
for kk in idx:
    ww = float(c[kk])
    if ww > 0 and np.isfinite(ww):
       p += (ww / wsum) * _lag_penalty(a, int(kk))
```
Good.

`_step_sequence`: ensures normalized.
```
def _step_sequence(params, n):
   p=float(np.clip(params[0], 0.02, 0.98))
   logr=float(np.clip(params[1], -4.0, 4.0))
   r=float(np.exp(logr))
   k=int(round(p*n))
   if k < 1: k=1
   if k > n-1: k=n-1
   a=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
   a[:k]=1.0
   a[k:]=r
   a = _normalize(a)
   if a is None: a=np.ones(n)/n
   return a
```
`_normalize` top-level defined.

```
def _small_obj(x,n):
   x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
   if x.size < n: x=np.pad(x,(0,n-x.size))
   elif x.size > n: x=x[:n]
   x=np.clip(x,-6,6)
   w=np.exp(x)
   s=w.sum()
   if s<=... return inf
   return _score(w/s)
```

```
def _small_search(deadline, max_n=10, rng=None):
   if rng is None: rng=np.random.default_rng(1)
   best_seq=None; best_score=np.inf
   for n in range(3, max_n+1):
       for st in range(6):
          if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10: break
          x0 = rng.uniform(0,1,n) + rng.normal(0,0.2,n)
          sc=_small_obj(x0,n)
          if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_seq=_normalize(np.exp(np.clip(x0,-6,6))/...)
          if HAVE_OPT:
             res=minimize(_small_obj, x0, args=(n,), method='Nelder-Mead', options={'maxiter':80,'xatol':1e-3,'fatol':1e-5})
             if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun) and res.fun < best_score:
                best_score=res.fun
                # convert res.x back to a
                xr=np.asarray(res.x,float).ravel()[:n] pad
                xr=np.clip(xr,-6,6); w=np.exp(xr); best_seq=w/w.sum(); best_score=_score(best_seq) # maybe res.fun approximate
       if best_score < 1.30: break
   return best_seq,best_score
```

```
def _exp_seq(x,n):
    x=np.asarray(x,float).ravel()
    if x.size < n: x=np.pad(x,(0,n-x.size))
    else: x=x[:n]
    x=np.clip(x,-8,8)

       return np.ones(n)/n
    return w/s
```

Implementation details:
```
def _local_search(a, deadline, rng):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a is None: return None, np.inf
    best = a.copy()
    best_score, _ = _score_normalized(best)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
       return best, best_score
    cur = a.copy()
    eta = 0.2
    fail = 0
    n = cur.size
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       sc, c = _score_normalized(cur)
       if not np.isfinite(sc): break
       if sc < best_score:
          best_score=sc; best=cur.copy()
       if c.size == 0: break
       p = _top_penalty(cur, c, 5)
       pmax = float(np.max(p)) if p.size else 0.0
       if pmax <= 1e-300:
          k = int(np.argmax(c))
          p = _lag_penalty(cur, k)
          pmax = float(np.max(p))
       if pmax <= 1e-300:
          fail += 1
          if fail > 50: break
          continue
       p = p / pmax
       improved = False
       # exponential candidates
       for e in (eta, 0.5*eta, 1.5*eta):
          e = float(np.clip(e, 0.01, 1.5))
          cand = cur * np.exp(-e * p)
          if rng.random() < 0.75:
             sig = 0.01 if best_score < 1.7 else 0.02
             cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
          cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-13, 1000.0)
          s = cand.sum()
          if s <= 1e-12: continue
          cand = cand / s
          sc_try, _ = _score_normalized(cand)
          if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score - 1e-12:
              cur = cand
              best = cur.copy()
              best_score = sc_try
              eta = e if e > 0.02 else eta
              improved = True
              fail = 0
              break
       # linear candidates if not improved
       if not improved:
          for alpha in (0.03,0.08):
             cand = cur - alpha * p
             if rng.random() < 0.5:
                cand += rng.normal(0.0, 1e-5 / max(1,n), n)
             cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
             cand = _normalize(cand)
             if cand is None: continue
             sc_try,_=_score_normalized(cand)
             if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score - 1e-12:
                cur=cand; best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc_try; improved=True; fail=0; break
       if not improved:
          eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.8)
          fail += 1
          if fail > 30:
             # random kick
             sig = 0.15 if best_score > 1.8 else 0.05
             cand = best * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
             cand = _normalize(cand)
             if cand is not None:
                sc_try,_=_score_normalized(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
                   cur=cand; best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc_try; fail=0; eta=0.2
             # even if kick not improve, reset fail to avoid too frequent kicks? 
             fail = 0
             eta=0.2
    return best, best_score
```
This can be expensive because current score computed each loop even if cur unchanged and fail many; but okay. If stuck, many loops. Time bounded.
Maybe use `cur` already normalized; after rejection, cur unchanged, so recomputing same c repeatedly. We could store c and skip if fail. But okay. To improve, if no improvement for 5 loops, use stored c? Simpler not.

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
    HAVE_OPT = True
except Exception:
    HAVE_OPT = False


TARGET = 1.5027


def _seq_sort_key(item):
    return item[0]


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return None
    return a / s


def _score_normalized(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    a = a / s

    if n < 128:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        m = 2 * n - 1
        L = 1
        while L < m:
            L <<= 1
        F = np.fft.fft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.ifft(F * F).real[:m]

    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf, c

    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return np.inf, c

    return float(2.0 * n * M), c


def _score(a):
    sc, _ = _score_normalized(a)
    return sc


def _lag_penalty(a, k):
    n = a.size
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, int(k) - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, int(k))
    if lo <= hi:
        j = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        p[lo:hi + 1] = a[k - j]
    return p


def _top_penalty(a, c, ntop=5):
    p = np.zeros(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    n = c.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    m = min(int(ntop), n)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
    wsum = float(np.sum(c[idx]))
    if wsum <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(wsum):
        return p

    for kk in idx:
        ww = float(c[kk])
        if ww > 0.0 and np.isfinite(ww):
            p += (ww / wsum) * _lag_penalty(a, int(kk))
    return p


def _exp_seq(x, n):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if x.size < int(n):
        x = np.pad(x, (0, int(n) - x.size))
    elif x.size > int(n):
        x = x[:int(n)]

    x = np.clip(x, -8.0, 8.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return np.ones(int(n), dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return w / s


def _small_obj(x, n):
    return _score(_exp_seq(x, int(n)))


def _small_search(deadline, max_n=10, rng=None):
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf

    for n in range(3, int(max_n) + 1):
        for st in range(6):
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 10.0:
                break

            x0 = rng.uniform(0.0, 1.0, n) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n)
            seq = _exp_seq(x0, n)
            sc = _score(seq)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = seq.copy()

            if HAVE_OPT:
                res = minimize(
                    _small_obj,
                    x0.copy(),
                    args=(n,),
                    method="Nelder-Mead",
                    options={"maxiter": 80, "xatol": 1e-3, "fatol": 1e-5}
                )
                if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun):
                    seq = _exp_seq(res.x, n)
                    sc = _score(seq)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        best_seq = seq.copy()

        if best_score < 1.30:
            break

    return best_seq, best_score


def _trig_sequence(theta, n, K):
    theta = np.asarray(theta, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    need = 2 * int(K)
    if theta.size < need:
        theta = np.pad(theta, (0, need - theta.size))
    elif theta.size > need:
        theta = theta[:need]

    amp = theta[:K].copy()
    ph = theta[K:need].copy()
    amp = np.clip(amp, -1.6, 1.6)
    ph = ph % (2.0 * np.pi)

    n = int(n)
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for j in range(int(K)):
        aj = float(amp[j])
        if abs(aj) > 1e-14:
            f += aj * np.sin((2.0 * np.pi * float(j + 1)) * x + float(ph[j]))

    f = _normalize(f)
    if f is None:
        f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return f


def _trig_obj(theta, n, K):
    return _score(_trig_sequence(theta, int(n), int(K)))


def _trig_search(deadline, rng, K=3):
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(777)

    K = int(K)
    n_eval = 256
    n_high = 512

    best_theta = np.zeros(2 * K, dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = np.inf

    starts = [best_theta.copy()]

    for amp in (-0.9, -0.6, -0.3, 0.3, 0.6, 0.9):
        th = np.zeros(2 * K, dtype=np.float64)
        th[0] = amp
        starts.append(th)

    for ph in (0.0, np.pi / 2.0, -np.pi / 2.0):
        th = np.zeros(2 * K, dtype=np.float64)
        th[0] = 0.7
        th[K] = ph
        starts.append(th)
        th2 = np.zeros(2 * K, dtype=np.float64)
        th2[0] = -0.7
        th2[K] = ph
        starts.append(th2)

    for i in range(18):
        th = np.concatenate((
            rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0, K),
            rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, K)
        ))
        starts.append(th)

    if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 5.0:
        seq = _trig_sequence(best_theta, 1024, K)
        return seq, _score(seq), best_theta

    for th0 in starts:
        sc = _trig_obj(th0, n_eval, K)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_theta = th0.copy()

    if HAVE_OPT:
        for th0 in starts[:12]:
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 30.0:
                break
            res = minimize(
                _trig_obj,
                th0.copy(),
                args=(n_eval, K),
                method="Nelder-Mead",
                options={"maxiter": 70, "xatol": 1e-3, "fatol": 1e-5}
            )
            if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun):
                th = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
                sc_low = _trig_obj(th, n_eval, K)
                sc_high = _trig_obj(th, n_high, K)
                if np.isfinite(sc_high) and sc_high < best_score:
                    best_score = sc_high
                    best_theta = th.copy()
                elif np.isfinite(sc_low) and sc_low < best_score:
                    best_score = sc_low
                    best_theta = th.copy()
    else:
        for _ in range(15000):
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 30.0:
                break
            th = np.concatenate((
                rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0, K),
                rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, K)
            ))
            sc = _trig_obj(th, n_eval, K)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_theta = th.copy()

    if HAVE_OPT and time.monotonic() < float(deadline) - 20.0:
        res = minimize(
            _trig_obj,
            best_theta.copy(),
            args=(n_high, K),
            method="Nelder-Mead",
            options={"maxiter": 120, "xatol": 1e-4, "fatol": 1e-6}
        )
        if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun):
            th = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            sc = _trig_obj(th, 1024, K)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_theta = th.copy()

    seq = _trig_sequence(best_theta, 1024, K)
    final = _score(seq)
    return seq, final, best_theta


def _step_sequence(params, n):
    n = int(n)
    p = float(np.clip(params[0], 0.02, 0.98))
    logr = float(np.clip(params[1], -4.0, 4.0))
    r = float(np.exp(logr))

    k = int(round(p * n))
    if k < 1:
        k = 1
    if k > n - 1:
        k = n - 1

    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:k] = 1.0
    a[k:] = r

    a = _normalize(a)
    if a is None:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return a


def _step_obj(params, n):
    return _score(_step_sequence(params, int(n)))


def _step_search(deadline, rng):
    if rng is None:
        rng = np.random.default_rng(999)

    n_eval = 256
    best_params = np.array([0.5, 0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = np.inf

    grid_p = np.linspace(0.05, 0.95, 19)
    grid_r = np.array([0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0])

    for p in grid_p:
        if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 5.0:
            break
        for r in grid_r:
            params = np.array([float(p), float(np.log(r))], dtype=np.float64)
            sc = _step_obj(params, n_eval)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_params = params.copy()

    if HAVE_OPT:
        starts = [best_params.copy()]
        for _ in range(8):
            starts.append(np.array([
                rng.uniform(0.1, 0.9),
                float(np.log(rng.uniform(0.2, 5.0)))
            ], dtype=np.float64))

        for st in starts:
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 10.0:
                break
            res = minimize(
                _step_obj,
                st.copy(),
                args=(n_eval,),
                method="Nelder-Mead",
                options={"maxiter": 100, "xatol": 1e-3, "fatol": 1e-5}
            )
            if res.x is not None and np.isfinite(res.fun):
                pm = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
                sc = _step_obj(pm, 512)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                    best_score = sc
                    best_params = pm
    else:
        for _ in range(5000):
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 10.0:
                break
            pm = np.array([
                rng.uniform(0.05, 0.95),
                float(np.log(rng.uniform(0.1, 6.0)))
            ], dtype=np.float64)
            sc = _step_obj(pm, n_eval)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_params = pm

    seq = _step_sequence(best_params, 1024)
    return seq, _score(seq), best_params


def _local_search(a, deadline, rng):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a is None:
        return None, np.inf

    cur = a.copy()
    best = a.copy()
    best_score, _ = _score_normalized(best)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best, best_score

    n = int(cur.size)
    eta = 0.20
    fail = 0

    while time.monotonic() < float(deadline):
        sc, c = _score_normalized(cur)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            break

        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best = cur.copy()

        if c.size == 0:
            break

        p = _top_penalty(cur, c, 5)
        pmax = float(np.max(p)) if p.size else 0.0
        if pmax <= 1e-300:
            k = int(np.argmax(c)) if c.size else 0
            p = _lag_penalty(cur, k)
            pmax = float(np.max(p)) if p.size else 0.0
        if pmax <= 1e-300:
            fail += 1
            if fail > 80:
                break
            continue

        p = p / pmax
        ok = False

        for e in (eta, 0.5 * eta, 1.5 * eta):
            e = float(np.clip(e, 0.01, 1.5))
            cand = cur * np.exp(-e * p)

            if rng.random() < 0.75:
                sig = 0.01 if best_score < 1.7 else 0.02
                cand *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))

            cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-13, 1000.0)
            s = float(np.sum(cand))
            if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
                continue
            cand = cand / s

            sc_try, _ = _score_normalized(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score - 1e-12:
                cur = cand
                best = cur.copy()
                best_score = sc_try
                eta = e if e > 0.02 else eta
                fail = 0
                ok = True
                break

        if not ok:
            for alpha in (0.03, 0.08):
                cand = cur - alpha * p
                if rng.random() < 0.45:
                    cand += rng.normal(0.0, 1e-5 / max(1.0, float(n)), n)
                cand = _normalize(cand)
                if cand is None:
                    continue

                sc_try, _ = _score_normalized(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score - 1e-12:
                    cur = cand
                    best = cur.copy()
                    best_score = sc_try
                    fail = 0
                    ok = True
                    break

        if not ok:
            eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.8)
            fail += 1
            if fail > 35:
                sig = 0.15 if best_score > 1.8 else 0.05
                cand = best * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n))
                cand = _normalize(cand)
                if cand is not None:
                    sc_try, _ = _score_normalized(cand)
                    if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < best_score:
                        cur = cand
                        best = cur.copy()
                        best_score = sc_try
                fail = 0
                eta = 0.20

    return best, best_score


def _interpolate_seq(a, n):
    a = _normalize(a)
    if a is None:
        return np.ones(int(n), dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    n = int(n)
    if a.size == n:
        return a
    if a.size == 1:
        return a[0] * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y = np.interp(xn, xo, a)
    y = _normalize(y)
    if y is None:
        y = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return y


def propose_candidate(*args, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = 1800.0
    if "budget_s" in kwargs:
        try:
            budget = float(kwargs["budget_s"])
        except Exception:
            budget = 1800.0
    budget = max(60.0, min(budget, 1800.0))
    deadline = start + max(30.0, budget - 18.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(20240527)

    best_seq = None
    best_score = np.inf
    trig_theta = None
    step_params = None

    print(f"starting search, budget={budget:.1f}s", flush=True)

    # Small exhaustive-ish search; if it finds something very good, we keep it.
    end_small = min(deadline - 220.0, start + 160.0)
    if end_small > time.monotonic() + 8.0:
        seq, sc = _small_search(end_small, max_n=10, rng=rng)
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq = seq
            best_score = sc
        print(f"small-n best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Step-parameter search, cheap diversity.
    end_step = min(deadline - 160.0, start + 260.0)
    if end_step > time.monotonic() + 8.0:
        seq, sc, pm = _step_search(end_step, rng)
        if pm is not None:
            step_params = pm
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq = seq
            best_score = sc
        print(f"step best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Low-dimensional trigonometric shape search.
    K = 3
    end_trig = min(deadline - 110.0, start + 560.0)
    if end_trig > time.monotonic() + 10.0:
        seq, sc, th = _trig_search(end_trig, rng, K=K)
        if th is not None:
            trig_theta = th
        if seq is not None and np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq = seq
            best_score = sc
        print(f"trig best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Build final-length candidates.
    N_FINAL = 6121
    candidates = []

    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size >= 2:
        candidates.append((float(best_score), np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()))

    if trig_theta is not None:
        seq = _trig_sequence(trig_theta, N_FINAL, K)
        sc = _score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((float(sc), seq))

    if step_params is not None:
        seq = _step_sequence(step_params, N_FINAL)
        sc = _score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((float(sc), seq))

    # A few deterministic fallback shapes.
    for th in (
        np.array([0.7, -0.35, 0.18, 0.0, 0.9, 2.1], dtype=np.float64),
        np.array([0.5, 0.6, -0.4, 0.0, 0.0, 1.7], dtype=np.float64),
        np.array([0.8, 0.0, 0.0, np.pi / 2.0, 0.0, 0.0], dtype=np.float64),
        np.array([0.0, 0.7, -0.7, 0.0, 0.0, 0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    ):
        seq = _trig_sequence(th, N_FINAL, K)
        sc = _score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            candidates.append((float(sc), seq))

    try:
        hs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if hs.ndim != 1:
            hs = hs.ravel()
        if hs.size >= 16:
            if hs.size == N_FINAL:
                cand = _normalize(hs)
            else:
                cand = _interpolate_seq(hs, N_FINAL)
            if cand is not None:
                sc = _score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    candidates.append((float(sc), cand))
    except Exception:
        pass

    if not candidates:
        candidates.append((2.0, np.ones(N_FINAL, dtype=np.float64)))

    candidates.sort(key=_seq_sort_key)

    print("top candidate scores before local:",
          " ".join(f"{s:.6f}" for s, _ in candidates[:5]), flush=True)

    # Local multiplicative/penalty search on the best few final candidates.
    local_time_left = max(0.0, float(deadline) - time.monotonic() - 30.0)
    if local_time_left > 80.0:
        take = min(3, len(candidates))
        for idx in range(take):
            if time.monotonic() > float(deadline) - 20.0:
                break
            sc0, seq0 = candidates[idx]
            remaining = max(0.0, float(deadline - 15.0) - time.monotonic())
             if remaining <= 20.0:
                break
            slice_end = min(
                float(deadline) - 10.0,
                time.monotonic() + max(45.0, remaining / float(take - idx))
            )
            seq1, sc1 = _local_search(seq0, slice_end, rng)
            print(f"local {idx}: {sc1:.6f}", flush=True)
            if seq1 is not None and np.isfinite(sc1) and sc1 < best_score:
                best_seq = seq1
                best_score = sc1

    # Final safety: if the best sequence is empty or invalid, use uniform.
    if best_seq is None or best_seq.size == 0:
        best_seq = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    best_seq = _normalize(best_seq)
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)

    final_list = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in best_seq]

    try:
        exact = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print(f"final evaluated score={exact:.6f}", flush=True)
        if not np.isfinite(exact):
            final_list = [1.0] * 64
    except Exception:
        print("evaluate_sequence call failed; returning un-evaluated best", flush=True)

    return final_list
```
